Replace 20 with the result of\n   path.getTotalLength() for your path; round caps mean any\n   sub-pixel overshoot is invisible. */\n.t-success-check svg path {\n  stroke-dasharray: 20;\n  stroke-dashoffset: 20;\n}\n\n.t-success-check[data-state=\"in\"] {\n  animation:\n    t-check-fade   var(--check-opacity-dur) var(--check-ease-opacity) forwards,\n    t-check-rotate var(--check-rotate-dur)  var(--check-ease-rotate)  forwards,\n    t-check-blur   var(--check-blur-dur)    var(--check-ease-out)     forwards,\n    t-check-bob    var(--check-bob-dur)     var(--check-ease-bob)     forwards;\n}\n.t-success-check[data-state=\"in\"] svg path {\n  animation: t-check-draw var(--check-path-dur) var(--check-ease-path) var(--check-path-delay, 0ms) forwards;\n}\n\n@keyframes t-check-fade { from { opacity: 0; } to { opacity: 1; } }\n@keyframes t-check-rotate {\n  from { transform: rotate(var(--check-rotate-from)); }\n  to   { transform: rotate(0deg); }\n}\n@keyframes t-check-blur {\n  from { filter: blur(var(--check-blur-from)); }\n  to   { filter: blur(0); }\n}\n@keyframes t-check-bob {\n  from { translate: 0 var(--check-y-amount); }\n  to   { translate: 0 0; }\n}\n@keyframes t-check-draw { to { stroke-dashoffset: 0; } }\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-success-check { animation: none !important; opacity: 1; }\n  .t-success-check svg path { animation: none !important; stroke-dashoffset: 0 !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p10\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p10\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// The .t-success-check wrapper is the API surface \u2014 drop any icon\n// (SVG, image, glyph) inside it. data-state drives the appear\n// transition: \"out\" is the resting hidden state (opacity 0, no\n// animation), \"in\" runs fade + rotate + blur + Y-bob + path draw\n// in parallel. To replay \"in\" cleanly on rapid re-triggers we clear\n// the state and re-apply it after a forced layout commit so the\n// keyframes restart from offset 0.\n//\n// This snippet covers the appear transition only \u2014 bring your own\n// hide behavior (unmount, opacity:0, or a custom exit).\nexport function SuccessCheck({ children }) {\n  const [state, setState] = useState(\"out\");\n  const ref = useRef(null);\n\n  const show = () => {\n    setState(\"out\");\n    requestAnimationFrame(() => {\n      if (ref.current) void ref.current.offsetWidth;\n      setState(\"in\");\n    });\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <>\n      <button type=\"button\" onClick={show}>Animate</button>\n      <span\n        ref={ref}\n        className=\"t-success-check\"\n        data-state={state}\n        aria-hidden=\"true\"\n      >\n        {children /* drop your SVG / icon here */}\n      </span>\n    </>\n  );\n}\n"
 },
 {
  "slug": "avatar-group-hover",
  "name": "Avatar group hover",
  "sub": "Distance-falloff lift with bouncy return",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Avatar group hover */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <!-- Apply .t-avatar to each item in your group (avatar,\n         chip, badge, button \u2014 anything). Bring your own size,\n         shape, and stacking; this stylesheet only owns the\n         hover transform + transition. -->\n    <div class=\"t-avatar-group\">\n      <div class=\"t-avatar\"><!-- your item --></div>\n      <div class=\"t-avatar\"><!-- your item --></div>\n      <!-- \u2026 -->\n    </div>\n\n  Wire-up (vanilla JS):\n    On `mouseenter` of any .t-avatar, walk every sibling and\n    set inline:\n      el.style.setProperty('--shift',\n        (lift * Math.pow(falloff, distance)).toFixed(3) + 'px');\n      el.style.setProperty('--scale-active',\n        i === activeIdx ? scale : 1);\n    Set transition-timing-function inline BEFORE the\n    variable writes \u2014 use --avatar-ease-in on hover-in and\n    --avatar-ease-out on the root's `mouseleave` (resets\n    --shift to 0 and --scale-active to 1).\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --avatar-lift: -4px;\n  --avatar-dur: 320ms;\n  --avatar-scale: 1.05;\n  --avatar-falloff: 0.45;\n  --avatar-ease-in: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --avatar-ease-out: cubic-bezier(0.34, 3.85, 0.64, 1);\n}\n\n/* Hover-spring transition only \u2014 bring your own avatar/chip\n   styling (size, shape, border, stacking, background). */\n.t-avatar {\n  transform-origin: center;\n  /* translateY before scale so scale doesn't amplify the lift offset. */\n  transform:\n    translateY(var(--shift, 0px))\n    scale(var(--scale-active, 1));\n  transition: transform var(--avatar-dur) var(--avatar-ease-in);\n  will-change: transform;\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-avatar { transition: none !important; transform: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Avatar group hover (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\nimport { useRef } from \"react\";\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --avatar-lift: -4px;\n  --avatar-dur: 320ms;\n  --avatar-scale: 1.05;\n  --avatar-falloff: 0.45;\n  --avatar-ease-in: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --avatar-ease-out: cubic-bezier(0.34, 3.85, 0.64, 1);\n}\n\n/* Hover-spring transition only \u2014 bring your own avatar/chip\n   styling (size, shape, border, stacking, background). */\n.t-avatar {\n  transform-origin: center;\n  /* translateY before scale so scale doesn't amplify the lift offset. */\n  transform:\n    translateY(var(--shift, 0px))\n    scale(var(--scale-active, 1));\n  transition: transform var(--avatar-dur) var(--avatar-ease-in);\n  will-change: transform;\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-avatar { transition: none !important; transform: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p11\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p11\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// `items` is any list of React nodes (avatars, chips, badges, etc.) \u2014\n// this hook only owns the hover-spring transition. Each item is wrapped\n// in a .t-avatar so it picks up the transform/transition rules from CSS.\n//\n// On mouseenter of any sibling, we write per-item:\n//   --shift         = lift * pow(falloff, distance from hovered)\n//   --scale-active  = scale on the hovered item, 1 elsewhere\n// Direction-aware easing: we set transition-timing-function inline\n// BEFORE the variable writes so the lift uses --avatar-ease-in\n// (clean) and the return uses --avatar-ease-out (bouncy spring).\nexport function AvatarGroup({ items }) {\n  const rootRef = useRef(null);\n\n  const setShifts = (activeIdx, phase) => {\n    if (!rootRef.current) return;\n    const cs = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement);\n    const num = (name, fb) => {\n      const v = parseFloat(cs.getPropertyValue(name));\n      return Number.isFinite(v) ? v : fb;\n    };\n    const ease = (name, fb) =>\n      cs.getPropertyValue(name).trim() || fb;\n\n    const lift    = num(\"--avatar-lift\", -4);\n    const falloff = num(\"--avatar-falloff\", 0.45);\n    const scale   = num(\"--avatar-scale\", 1.05);\n    const tf      = phase === \"out\"\n      ? ease(\"--avatar-ease-out\", \"cubic-bezier(0.34, 3.85, 0.64, 1)\")\n      : ease(\"--avatar-ease-in\",  \"cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)\");\n\n    const els = rootRef.current.querySelectorAll(\".t-avatar\");\n    els.forEach((el, i) => {\n      el.style.transitionTimingFunction = tf;\n      if (activeIdx == null) {\n        el.style.setProperty(\"--shift\", \"0px\");\n        el.style.setProperty(\"--scale-active\", \"1\");\n        return;\n      }\n      const d = Math.abs(i - activeIdx);\n      el.style.setProperty(\n        \"--shift\",\n        (lift * Math.pow(falloff, d)).toFixed(3) + \"px\"\n      );\n      el.style.setProperty(\n        \"--scale-active\",\n        i === activeIdx ? String(scale) : \"1\"\n      );\n    });\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div\n      ref={rootRef}\n      onMouseLeave={() => setShifts(null, \"out\")}\n    >\n      {items.map((node, i) => (\n        <div\n          key={i}\n          className=\"t-avatar\"\n          onMouseEnter={() => setShifts(i, \"in\")}\n        >\n          {node}\n        </div>\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"
 },
 {
  "slug": "error-state-shake",
  "name": "Error state shake",
  "sub": "Cubic-bezier shake on error",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Error state shake */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <!-- Apply .t-input-wrap to your wrapper, .t-input to the\n         element that should shake (your input field, its\n         bordered wrapper \u2014 whatever owns the visible border),\n         and .t-error-msg to the message you want to reveal.\n         Bring your own sizing, padding, border colors, and\n         typography. -->\n    <div class=\"t-input-wrap\">\n      <div class=\"t-input\">\n        <input type=\"text\">\n      </div>\n      <p class=\"t-error-msg\">Please enter a valid email.</p>\n    </div>\n\n  Trigger:\n    - Add `.is-error` to .t-input-wrap and .t-input. Your\n      own border-color rules drive the visible color; this\n      stylesheet only owns the tween.\n    - Restart the shake by removing `.is-shaking` from\n      .t-input, forcing a reflow, then re-adding it.\n    - Optional: after --revert-hold ms, drop both\n      `.is-error` classes so border + message fade back\n      to neutral over --revert-dur.\n\n  Per-segment ease: each keyframe stop carries its own\n  animation-timing-function so each leg follows the Figma\n  cubic-bezier curve independently.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --shake-distance: 6px;\n  --shake-overshoot: 4px;\n  --shake-dur-a: 80ms;\n  --shake-dur-b: 60ms;\n  --shake-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --revert-hold: 3000ms;\n  --revert-dur: 280ms;\n}\n\n/* Border-color tween. Define your input's default / focused\n   / error border-color in your own component CSS \u2014 this rule\n   only owns the interpolation. Use a constant border-width\n   across states so the tween never shifts inner content. */\n.t-input {\n  transition: border-color 150ms ease-out;\n  will-change: transform;\n}\n.t-input.is-error {\n  /* Error border auto-reverts on the hold timer, so the\n     fade-out uses the slower revert duration (matches the\n     message fade). */\n  transition: border-color var(--revert-dur, 280ms) ease-out;\n}\n\n/* Error message reveal. Visibility is delayed by --revert-dur\n   on hide so the message stays painted for the full opacity\n   fade-out. Entering .is-error drops the delay to 0 so the\n   message becomes visible immediately. */\n.t-error-msg {\n  opacity: 0;\n  visibility: hidden;\n  transition:\n    opacity    var(--revert-dur, 280ms) ease-out,\n    visibility 0s linear var(--revert-dur, 280ms);\n}\n.t-input-wrap.is-error .t-error-msg {\n  opacity: 1;\n  visibility: visible;\n  transition:\n    opacity    var(--revert-dur, 280ms) ease-out,\n    visibility 0s linear 0s;\n}\n\n/* Multi-segment keyframe with per-stop easing so each leg\n   of the shake follows its own cubic-bezier independently.\n   %-stops are cumulative durations as a fraction of the\n   total (80, 60, 80, 60 = 280ms): 28.57%, 57.14%, 78.57%,\n   100%. Recompute if any segment duration changes. */\n.t-input.is-shaking {\n  animation: t-input-shake calc(\n      var(--shake-dur-a) * 2 + var(--shake-dur-b) * 2\n    ) linear;\n}\n@keyframes t-input-shake {\n  0%      { transform: translateX(0);                                 animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  28.57%  { transform: translateX(var(--shake-distance));             animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  57.14%  { transform: translateX(calc(var(--shake-distance) * -1)); animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  78.57%  { transform: translateX(var(--shake-overshoot));            animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  100%    { transform: translateX(0); }\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-input { animation: none !important; transform: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Error state shake (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\nimport { useRef, useState } from \"react\";\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --shake-distance: 6px;\n  --shake-overshoot: 4px;\n  --shake-dur-a: 80ms;\n  --shake-dur-b: 60ms;\n  --shake-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --revert-hold: 3000ms;\n  --revert-dur: 280ms;\n}\n\n/* Border-color tween. Define your input's default / focused\n   / error border-color in your own component CSS \u2014 this rule\n   only owns the interpolation. Use a constant border-width\n   across states so the tween never shifts inner content. */\n.t-input {\n  transition: border-color 150ms ease-out;\n  will-change: transform;\n}\n.t-input.is-error {\n  /* Error border auto-reverts on the hold timer, so the\n     fade-out uses the slower revert duration (matches the\n     message fade). */\n  transition: border-color var(--revert-dur, 280ms) ease-out;\n}\n\n/* Error message reveal. Visibility is delayed by --revert-dur\n   on hide so the message stays painted for the full opacity\n   fade-out. Entering .is-error drops the delay to 0 so the\n   message becomes visible immediately. */\n.t-error-msg {\n  opacity: 0;\n  visibility: hidden;\n  transition:\n    opacity    var(--revert-dur, 280ms) ease-out,\n    visibility 0s linear var(--revert-dur, 280ms);\n}\n.t-input-wrap.is-error .t-error-msg {\n  opacity: 1;\n  visibility: visible;\n  transition:\n    opacity    var(--revert-dur, 280ms) ease-out,\n    visibility 0s linear 0s;\n}\n\n/* Multi-segment keyframe with per-stop easing so each leg\n   of the shake follows its own cubic-bezier independently.\n   %-stops are cumulative durations as a fraction of the\n   total (80, 60, 80, 60 = 280ms): 28.57%, 57.14%, 78.57%,\n   100%. Recompute if any segment duration changes. */\n.t-input.is-shaking {\n  animation: t-input-shake calc(\n      var(--shake-dur-a) * 2 + var(--shake-dur-b) * 2\n    ) linear;\n}\n@keyframes t-input-shake {\n  0%      { transform: translateX(0);                                 animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  28.57%  { transform: translateX(var(--shake-distance));             animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  57.14%  { transform: translateX(calc(var(--shake-distance) * -1)); animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  78.57%  { transform: translateX(var(--shake-overshoot));            animation-timing-function: var(--shake-ease); }\n  100%    { transform: translateX(0); }\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-input { animation: none !important; transform: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p12\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p12\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// This hook owns ONLY the shake + error-state wiring; the input\n// markup, message text, sizing, and border colors are yours.\n//\n// Wire it in as:\n//   <InputShake message=\"Please enter a valid email.\">\n//     <input type=\"text\" onInput={onUserEdit} />\n//   </InputShake>\n//\n// `Animate` always triggers the error state \u2014 this is a demo, not\n// real validation. The shake replay pattern is the same as the rest\n// of the gallery: drop the class, force a reflow, re-add it. We\n// then auto-revert after --revert-hold so the demo self-resets.\n// Pass an `onCancel` if you want to clear the error early when the\n// user starts editing (e.g. on the input's `onInput` event).\nexport function InputShake({ children, message, onCancel }) {\n  const inputRef = useRef(null);\n  const timerRef = useRef(null);\n  const [error, setError] = useState(false);\n\n  const trigger = () => {\n    if (!inputRef.current) return;\n    setError(true);\n    inputRef.current.classList.remove(\"is-shaking\");\n    void inputRef.current.offsetWidth;\n    inputRef.current.classList.add(\"is-shaking\");\n\n    if (timerRef.current) window.clearTimeout(timerRef.current);\n    const shakeMs =\n      readMs(\"--shake-dur-a\", 80) * 2 +\n      readMs(\"--shake-dur-b\", 60) * 2;\n    const hold = readMs(\"--revert-hold\", 3000);\n    timerRef.current = window.setTimeout(() => {\n      setError(false);\n      timerRef.current = null;\n    }, shakeMs + hold);\n  };\n\n  // Call this from your input's onInput / onChange handler if you\n  // want typing to cancel the auto-revert and clear the error.\n  const cancel = () => {\n    if (timerRef.current) {\n      window.clearTimeout(timerRef.current);\n      timerRef.current = null;\n    }\n    setError(false);\n    onCancel?.();\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <>\n      <button type=\"button\" onClick={trigger}>Animate</button>\n      <div className={\"t-input-wrap\" + (error ? \" is-error\" : \"\")}>\n        <div\n          ref={inputRef}\n          className={\"t-input\" + (error ? \" is-error\" : \"\")}\n          onInput={cancel}\n        >\n          {children}\n        </div>\n        <p className=\"t-error-msg\">{message}</p>\n      </div>\n    </>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction readMs(name, fallback) {\n  const raw = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)\n    .getPropertyValue(name).trim();\n  const n = parseFloat(raw);\n  return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : fallback;\n}\n"
 },

 {
  "slug": "input-clear-with-dissolve",
  "name": "Input clear with dissolve",
  "sub": "Clear with per-word dissolve",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Input clear with dissolve */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <!-- Drop .t-clear on a wrapper that you've sized like an\n         input field (positioning, padding, border, radius are\n         yours). Inside it stack a real <input>, a mirror that\n         visualizes the value, a fake placeholder for the new\n         empty state, and a glow layer that gets the per-word\n         radial-gradient stack written into it from JS. -->\n    <div class=\"t-clear has-value\">\n      <input type=\"text\" value=\"\u2026\" />\n      <div class=\"t-clear-mirror\" aria-hidden=\"true\">\u2026</div>\n      <div class=\"t-clear-placeholder\" aria-hidden=\"true\">Search</div>\n      <div class=\"t-clear-glow\" aria-hidden=\"true\"></div>\n      <button class=\"t-clear-btn\" aria-label=\"Clear\">\u2026</button>\n    </div>\n\n  Pair with a small JS routine (mirrors the gallery code) that\n  flips `.is-clearing`, animates the mirror's translateY/opacity\n  per frame, mirrors the math on the placeholder, and writes a\n  stack of `radial-gradient(...)` layers onto .t-clear-glow's\n  background so each word gets its own streak. Per-frame JS is\n  unavoidable: the streak's rise/peak/fall envelope cannot be\n  expressed as a static @keyframe.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --clear-dur: 1000ms;\n  --clear-out-dur: 400ms;\n  --clear-in-dur: 400ms;\n  --clear-out-fly: 12px;\n  --clear-in-fly: 12px;\n  --clear-out-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --clear-in-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --clear-blur: 2px;\n  --glow-delay: 50ms;\n  --glow-peak-at: 0.15;\n  --glow-opacity: 0.42;\n  --glow-spread: 1.5;\n}\n\n/* The wrap clips the glow to its rounded box. The hairline\n   border is `inset` so it sits inside that clip \u2014 when the\n   glow's mix-blend-mode darkens its area, the border\n   underneath darkens with it. Bring your own width / height /\n   border-radius / surface color. */\n.t-clear {\n  position: relative;\n  overflow: hidden;\n}\n.t-clear-mirror,\n.t-clear-placeholder {\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n  display: flex;\n  align-items: center;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  white-space: nowrap;\n  overflow: hidden;\n  z-index: 2;\n}\n.t-clear-mirror { opacity: 0; }\n.t-clear.has-value .t-clear-mirror,\n.t-clear.is-clearing .t-clear-mirror { opacity: 1; }\n/* Hide the input's own glyphs while the mirror owns them so\n   the cleared text doesn't double-render with the fly-up. */\n.t-clear.has-value > input,\n.t-clear.is-clearing > input {\n  -webkit-text-fill-color: transparent;\n}\n.t-clear.has-value .t-clear-placeholder { opacity: 0; }\n/* The streak overlay: empty by default; JS writes a stack of\n   `radial-gradient(...)` layers into background during a clear,\n   then animates opacity. mix-blend-mode: multiply darkens the\n   underlying input + hairline; flip to `screen` in dark mode\n   so the same alpha values lighten instead of vanish. */\n.t-clear-glow {\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  opacity: 0;\n  z-index: 3;\n  mix-blend-mode: multiply;\n}\n\n/* The transitions live in JS (per-frame transform/opacity/\n   filter writes), so this stylesheet only owns the resting\n   state + the variables that JS reads. Read them with\n   `parseFloat(getComputedStyle(root).getPropertyValue(...))`\n   so live tweaks apply on the next clear without a reload. */\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-clear-glow { opacity: 0 !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Input clear with dissolve (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\nimport { useEffect, useRef } from \"react\";\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --clear-dur: 1000ms;\n  --clear-out-dur: 400ms;\n  --clear-in-dur: 400ms;\n  --clear-out-fly: 12px;\n  --clear-in-fly: 12px;\n  --clear-out-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --clear-in-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --clear-blur: 2px;\n  --glow-delay: 50ms;\n  --glow-peak-at: 0.15;\n  --glow-opacity: 0.42;\n  --glow-spread: 1.5;\n}\n\n/* The wrap clips the glow to its rounded box. The hairline\n   border is `inset` so it sits inside that clip \u2014 when the\n   glow's mix-blend-mode darkens its area, the border\n   underneath darkens with it. Bring your own width / height /\n   border-radius / surface color. */\n.t-clear {\n  position: relative;\n  overflow: hidden;\n}\n.t-clear-mirror,\n.t-clear-placeholder {\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n  display: flex;\n  align-items: center;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  white-space: nowrap;\n  overflow: hidden;\n  z-index: 2;\n}\n.t-clear-mirror { opacity: 0; }\n.t-clear.has-value .t-clear-mirror,\n.t-clear.is-clearing .t-clear-mirror { opacity: 1; }\n/* Hide the input's own glyphs while the mirror owns them so\n   the cleared text doesn't double-render with the fly-up. */\n.t-clear.has-value > input,\n.t-clear.is-clearing > input {\n  -webkit-text-fill-color: transparent;\n}\n.t-clear.has-value .t-clear-placeholder { opacity: 0; }\n/* The streak overlay: empty by default; JS writes a stack of\n   `radial-gradient(...)` layers into background during a clear,\n   then animates opacity. mix-blend-mode: multiply darkens the\n   underlying input + hairline; flip to `screen` in dark mode\n   so the same alpha values lighten instead of vanish. */\n.t-clear-glow {\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  opacity: 0;\n  z-index: 3;\n  mix-blend-mode: multiply;\n}\n\n/* The transitions live in JS (per-frame transform/opacity/\n   filter writes), so this stylesheet only owns the resting\n   state + the variables that JS reads. Read them with\n   `parseFloat(getComputedStyle(root).getPropertyValue(...))`\n   so live tweaks apply on the next clear without a reload. */\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-clear-glow { opacity: 0 !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p13\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p13\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// Per-frame JS is unavoidable here: the per-word streak's\n// rise \u2192 peak \u2192 fall envelope can't be expressed as a static\n// @keyframe, so we tween transform/opacity/filter ourselves.\n//\n// Use it as:\n//   <ClearInput defaultValue=\"How do transitions work?\"\n//               placeholder=\"Search anything\" />\n//\n// Bring your own search icon, container size, and theming.\nexport function ClearInput({ defaultValue = \"\", placeholder = \"\" }) {\n  const wrapRef    = useRef(null);\n  const inputRef   = useRef(null);\n  const mirrorRef  = useRef(null);\n  const fakePhRef  = useRef(null);\n  const glowRef    = useRef(null);\n  const isClearing = useRef(false);\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const wrap = wrapRef.current;\n    const input = inputRef.current;\n    if (!wrap || !input) return;\n    const sync = () => {\n      const has = input.value.length > 0;\n      wrap.classList.toggle(\"has-value\", has);\n      if (has && mirrorRef.current) {\n        mirrorRef.current.textContent = input.value.replace(/ /g, \"\\u00a0\");\n      }\n    };\n    input.addEventListener(\"input\", sync);\n    sync();\n    return () => input.removeEventListener(\"input\", sync);\n  }, []);\n\n  const onClear = () => {\n    const wrap = wrapRef.current;\n    const input = inputRef.current;\n    const mirror = mirrorRef.current;\n    const fakePh = fakePhRef.current;\n    const glow = glowRef.current;\n    if (!wrap || !input || !mirror || !fakePh || !glow) return;\n    if (isClearing.current || !input.value) return;\n    isClearing.current = true;\n    const wasFocused = document.activeElement === input;\n    mirror.textContent = input.value.replace(/ /g, \"\\u00a0\");\n    const bg = buildLayers(wrap, mirror.textContent);\n    const peakAt   = readNum(\"--glow-peak-at\", 0.15);\n    const opacity  = readNum(\"--glow-opacity\", 0.42);\n    const total    = readNum(\"--clear-dur\", 1000);\n    const outDur   = readNum(\"--clear-out-dur\", 400);\n    const inDur    = readNum(\"--clear-in-dur\", 400);\n    const outFly   = readNum(\"--clear-out-fly\", 12);\n    const inFly    = readNum(\"--clear-in-fly\", 12);\n    const blurPx   = readNum(\"--clear-blur\", 2);\n    const glowDly  = readNum(\"--glow-delay\", 50);\n    const eOut = makeEase(readEase(\"--clear-out-ease\", \"cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)\"));\n    const eIn  = makeEase(readEase(\"--clear-in-ease\",  \"cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1)\"));\n\n    input.value = \"\";\n    wrap.classList.remove(\"has-value\");\n    wrap.classList.add(\"is-clearing\");\n    fakePh.style.transform = `translateY(-${inFly}px)`;\n    fakePh.style.opacity = \"0.9\";\n    fakePh.style.filter = `blur(${blurPx}px)`;\n    glow.style.background = bg;\n    glow.style.opacity = \"0\";\n\n    const start = performance.now();\n    const tick = (now) => {\n      const elapsed = now - start;\n      const p  = Math.min(1, elapsed / total);\n      const e  = eOut(Math.min(1, elapsed / outDur));\n      mirror.style.transform = `translateY(${(e * outFly).toFixed(1)}px)`;\n      mirror.style.opacity = (1 - e).toFixed(3);\n      mirror.style.filter = `blur(${(e * blurPx).toFixed(1)}px)`;\n      const pe = eIn(Math.min(1, elapsed / inDur));\n      fakePh.style.transform = `translateY(${(-inFly + pe * inFly).toFixed(1)}px)`;\n      fakePh.style.opacity = (0.9 + pe * 0.1).toFixed(3);\n      fakePh.style.filter = `blur(${(blurPx - pe * blurPx).toFixed(1)}px)`;\n      let g = 0;\n      if (elapsed > glowDly) {\n        const remaining = Math.max(1, total - glowDly);\n        const gp = Math.min(1, (elapsed - glowDly) / remaining);\n        g = gp < peakAt ? gp / peakAt : 1 - (gp - peakAt) / (1 - peakAt);\n      }\n      glow.style.opacity = (g * opacity).toFixed(3);\n      if (p < 1) requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n      else {\n        wrap.classList.remove(\"is-clearing\");\n        for (const el of [mirror, fakePh]) el.style.cssText = \"\";\n        mirror.textContent = \"\";\n        glow.style.opacity = \"0\";\n        glow.style.background = \"\";\n        isClearing.current = false;\n        if (wasFocused) input.focus({ preventScroll: true });\n      }\n    };\n    requestAnimationFrame(tick);\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <div ref={wrapRef} className=\"t-clear\">\n      <input\n        ref={inputRef}\n        type=\"text\"\n        defaultValue={defaultValue}\n        placeholder={placeholder}\n      />\n      <div ref={mirrorRef} className=\"t-clear-mirror\" aria-hidden=\"true\" />\n      <div ref={fakePhRef} className=\"t-clear-placeholder\" aria-hidden=\"true\">\n        {placeholder}\n      </div>\n      <div ref={glowRef} className=\"t-clear-glow\" aria-hidden=\"true\" />\n      <button\n        type=\"button\"\n        className=\"t-clear-btn\"\n        aria-label=\"Clear\"\n        // Trap focus on the input across pointer + mouse so the button\n        // never steals it during the clear animation.\n        onPointerDown={(e) => { if (document.activeElement === inputRef.current) e.preventDefault(); }}\n        onMouseDown={(e) => { if (document.activeElement === inputRef.current) e.preventDefault(); }}\n        onClick={onClear}\n      >\u00d7</button>\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction buildLayers(wrap, text) {\n  const inputW = wrap.clientWidth || 280;\n  const padLeft = 32;\n  const segments = text.split(/(\\s+)/);\n  const spread = readNum(\"--glow-spread\", 1.5);\n  const ctx = (buildLayers._ctx ||= (() => {\n    const c = document.createElement(\"canvas\").getContext(\"2d\");\n    c.font = \"400 13px Inter, sans-serif\";\n    return c;\n  })());\n  // Light mode paints black gradients (multiply blend); dark mode\n  // paints white (screen blend). Pair this with the matching\n  // `mix-blend-mode` rule on .t-clear-glow in your theme CSS.\n  const isDark = document.documentElement.getAttribute(\"data-theme\") === \"dark\";\n  const rgb = isDark ? \"255,255,255\" : \"0,0,0\";\n  const layers = [];\n  let x = 0;\n  for (const seg of segments) {\n    const w = ctx.measureText(seg).width;\n    if (seg.trim()) {\n      const cx = padLeft + x + w / 2;\n      const hw = Math.max(w * 0.45, 8) * spread;\n      const stops = [\n        { dx: 0,         rw: hw * 0.8,  rh: 7, a: 0.22 },\n        { dx: hw * 0.45, rw: hw * 0.55, rh: 8, a: 0.18 },\n        { dx: -hw * 0.4, rw: hw * 0.65, rh: 6, a: 0.16 },\n        { dx: hw * 0.15, rw: hw * 0.9,  rh: 5, a: 0.14 },\n      ];\n      for (const l of stops) {\n        const lx = ((cx + l.dx) / inputW * 100).toFixed(2);\n        layers.push(\n          `radial-gradient(ellipse ${Math.max(l.rw, 2).toFixed(1)}px ${l.rh}px at ${lx}% 100%, rgba(${rgb},${l.a.toFixed(3)}), transparent)`\n        );\n      }\n    }\n    x += w;\n  }\n  return layers.join(\", \");\n}\nfunction readNum(name, fb) {\n  const v = parseFloat(getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue(name));\n  return Number.isFinite(v) ? v : fb;\n}\nfunction readEase(name, fb) {\n  const v = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement).getPropertyValue(name).trim();\n  return v || fb;\n}\nfunction makeEase(ease) {\n  const m = ease.match(/cubic-bezier\\s*\\(\\s*([-\\d.]+)\\s*,\\s*([-\\d.]+)\\s*,\\s*([-\\d.]+)\\s*,\\s*([-\\d.]+)\\s*\\)/i);\n  if (!m) return (t) => t;\n  const [x1, y1, x2, y2] = [m[1], m[2], m[3], m[4]].map(parseFloat);\n  const cx = 3 * x1, bx = 3 * (x2 - x1) - cx, ax = 1 - cx - bx;\n  const cy = 3 * y1, by = 3 * (y2 - y1) - cy, ay = 1 - cy - by;\n  const sX = (s) => ((ax * s + bx) * s + cx) * s;\n  const sY = (s) => ((ay * s + by) * s + cy) * s;\n  const dX = (s) => (3 * ax * s + 2 * bx) * s + cx;\n  return (t) => {\n    if (t <= 0) return 0;\n    if (t >= 1) return 1;\n    let s = t;\n    for (let i = 0; i < 8; i++) {\n      const dx = sX(s) - t;\n      if (Math.abs(dx) < 1e-6) break;\n      const d = dX(s);\n      if (d === 0) break;\n      s -= dx / d;\n    }\n    return sY(s);\n  };\n}\n"
 },
 {
  "slug": "skeleton-loader-and-reveal",
  "name": "Skeleton loader and reveal",
  "sub": "Pulse to content cross-fade",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Skeleton loader and reveal */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <div class=\"t-skel\" data-state=\"loading\">\n      <div class=\"t-skel-skeleton is-pulsing\">\u2026</div>\n      <div class=\"t-skel-content\">\u2026</div>\n    </div>\n\n  State:\n    - Mount with `.is-pulsing` on the skeleton so it pulses\n      --pulse-count times.\n    - When data arrives, add `.is-revealed` to .t-skel \u2014 the\n      skeleton fades out + blurs and the content fades in +\n      un-blurs over --reveal-dur.\n    - To replay the loading state without animating the\n      reverse: add `.is-resetting` to .t-skel, remove\n      `.is-revealed`, force a reflow, then drop `.is-resetting`.\n\n  Bring your own avatar / text / wrapping. The skeleton stays\n  in the same flex slot as the content so the swap is\n  layout-free.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --pulse-dur: 1000ms;\n  --pulse-count: 1;\n  --pulse-min: 0.5;\n  --reveal-dur: 400ms;\n  --reveal-blur: 2px;\n  --reveal-ease: ease-in-out;\n}\n\n/* The wrap stacks two layers on the same coordinates. The\n   skeleton owns the cold pulse + the fade-out side of the\n   reveal; the content owns the fade-in side. They share the\n   same duration / ease so the swap reads as one motion. */\n.t-skel { position: relative; }\n.t-skel-skeleton,\n.t-skel-content {\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n}\n\n.t-skel-skeleton {\n  z-index: 1;\n  opacity: 1;\n  filter: blur(0);\n  transition:\n    opacity var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease),\n    filter  var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease);\n}\n.t-skel-content {\n  z-index: 2;\n  opacity: 0;\n  filter: blur(var(--reveal-blur));\n  transition:\n    opacity var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease),\n    filter  var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease);\n}\n.t-skel.is-revealed .t-skel-skeleton {\n  opacity: 0;\n  filter: blur(var(--reveal-blur));\n}\n.t-skel.is-revealed .t-skel-content {\n  opacity: 1;\n  filter: blur(0);\n}\n/* Snap-back when replaying: kill transitions so the reverse\n   (revealed \u2192 skeleton) is instant. Drop `.is-resetting`\n   after a forced reflow and the next reveal animates again. */\n.t-skel.is-resetting .t-skel-skeleton,\n.t-skel.is-resetting .t-skel-content {\n  transition: none !important;\n}\n\n/* Pulse: place the animation on the bar/avatar children, not\n   on the skeleton itself, so the skeleton's opacity / filter\n   stay free for the cross-fade transition above. */\n.t-skel-skeleton.is-pulsing > * {\n  animation: t-skel-pulse var(--pulse-dur) ease-in-out var(--pulse-count);\n}\n@keyframes t-skel-pulse {\n  0%, 100% { opacity: 1; }\n  50%      { opacity: var(--pulse-min); }\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-skel-skeleton, .t-skel-content {\n    transition: none !important;\n  }\n  .t-skel-skeleton.is-pulsing > * { animation: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Skeleton loader and reveal (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\nimport { useRef, useState } from \"react\";\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --pulse-dur: 1000ms;\n  --pulse-count: 1;\n  --pulse-min: 0.5;\n  --reveal-dur: 400ms;\n  --reveal-blur: 2px;\n  --reveal-ease: ease-in-out;\n}\n\n/* The wrap stacks two layers on the same coordinates. The\n   skeleton owns the cold pulse + the fade-out side of the\n   reveal; the content owns the fade-in side. They share the\n   same duration / ease so the swap reads as one motion. */\n.t-skel { position: relative; }\n.t-skel-skeleton,\n.t-skel-content {\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n}\n\n.t-skel-skeleton {\n  z-index: 1;\n  opacity: 1;\n  filter: blur(0);\n  transition:\n    opacity var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease),\n    filter  var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease);\n}\n.t-skel-content {\n  z-index: 2;\n  opacity: 0;\n  filter: blur(var(--reveal-blur));\n  transition:\n    opacity var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease),\n    filter  var(--reveal-dur) var(--reveal-ease);\n}\n.t-skel.is-revealed .t-skel-skeleton {\n  opacity: 0;\n  filter: blur(var(--reveal-blur));\n}\n.t-skel.is-revealed .t-skel-content {\n  opacity: 1;\n  filter: blur(0);\n}\n/* Snap-back when replaying: kill transitions so the reverse\n   (revealed \u2192 skeleton) is instant. Drop `.is-resetting`\n   after a forced reflow and the next reveal animates again. */\n.t-skel.is-resetting .t-skel-skeleton,\n.t-skel.is-resetting .t-skel-content {\n  transition: none !important;\n}\n\n/* Pulse: place the animation on the bar/avatar children, not\n   on the skeleton itself, so the skeleton's opacity / filter\n   stay free for the cross-fade transition above. */\n.t-skel-skeleton.is-pulsing > * {\n  animation: t-skel-pulse var(--pulse-dur) ease-in-out var(--pulse-count);\n}\n@keyframes t-skel-pulse {\n  0%, 100% { opacity: 1; }\n  50%      { opacity: var(--pulse-min); }\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-skel-skeleton, .t-skel-content {\n    transition: none !important;\n  }\n  .t-skel-skeleton.is-pulsing > * { animation: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p14\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p14\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// `loading` is yours to drive (data fetch, route change, etc.).\n// On every fresh load \u2192 ready cycle, call `replay()` to flash the\n// skeleton again before revealing the content.\nexport function SkeletonReveal({ skeleton, children }) {\n  const wrapRef = useRef(null);\n  const skelRef = useRef(null);\n  const timerRef = useRef(null);\n  const [revealed, setRevealed] = useState(false);\n\n  const replay = () => {\n    const wrap = wrapRef.current;\n    const skel = skelRef.current;\n    if (!wrap || !skel) return;\n    if (timerRef.current) window.clearTimeout(timerRef.current);\n    // Snap-back: kill transitions for one frame so the reverse\n    // (revealed \u2192 skeleton) is instant.\n    wrap.classList.add(\"is-resetting\");\n    setRevealed(false);\n    skel.classList.remove(\"is-pulsing\");\n    void skel.offsetWidth;\n    wrap.classList.remove(\"is-resetting\");\n    skel.classList.add(\"is-pulsing\");\n    const dur = readMs(\"--pulse-dur\", 1000);\n    const count = parseFloat(\n      getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)\n        .getPropertyValue(\"--pulse-count\")\n    ) || 1;\n    timerRef.current = window.setTimeout(() => {\n      setRevealed(true);\n      timerRef.current = null;\n    }, dur * count);\n  };\n\n  return (\n    <>\n      <button type=\"button\" onClick={replay}>Animate</button>\n      <div\n        ref={wrapRef}\n        className={\"t-skel\" + (revealed ? \" is-revealed\" : \"\")}\n      >\n        <div ref={skelRef} className=\"t-skel-skeleton is-pulsing\" aria-hidden=\"true\">\n          {skeleton}\n        </div>\n        <div className=\"t-skel-content\">{children}</div>\n      </div>\n    </>\n  );\n}\n\nfunction readMs(name, fallback) {\n  const raw = getComputedStyle(document.documentElement)\n    .getPropertyValue(name).trim();\n  const n = parseFloat(raw);\n  return Number.isFinite(n) ? n : fallback;\n}\n"

 {
  "slug": "texts-reveal",
  "name": "Texts reveal",
  "sub": "Two lines rise with offset stagger",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Texts reveal */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <div class=\"t-stagger\">\n      <strong class=\"t-stagger-line t-stagger-line--1\">\u2026</strong>\n      <span class=\"t-stagger-line t-stagger-line--2\">\u2026</span>\n    </div>\n\n  State:\n    - Add `.is-shown` to play the staggered entrance.\n    - Add `.is-hiding` (and remove `.is-shown`) to fade\n      out in place over a short 200ms \u2014 independent of the\n      entrance timing so the exit doesn't replay the stagger.\n\n  Add more lines by adding `.t-stagger-line--N` with\n  `transition-delay: calc(var(--stagger-stagger) * (N - 1))`.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --stagger-dur: 500ms;\n  --stagger-distance: 12px;\n  --stagger-stagger: 40ms;\n  --stagger-blur: 3px;\n  --stagger-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n}\n\n/* Lines start translated down + blurred + invisible; .is-shown\n   on the parent flips them to their resting state. The second\n   line's transition-delay holds it back by --stagger-stagger\n   so the eye lands on the headline first. */\n.t-stagger-line {\n  display: block;\n  opacity: 0;\n  transform: translateY(var(--stagger-distance));\n  filter: blur(var(--stagger-blur));\n  transition:\n    opacity   var(--stagger-dur) var(--stagger-ease),\n    transform var(--stagger-dur) var(--stagger-ease),\n    filter    var(--stagger-dur) var(--stagger-ease);\n  will-change: transform, opacity, filter;\n}\n.t-stagger-line--2 { transition-delay: var(--stagger-stagger); }\n\n.t-stagger.is-shown .t-stagger-line {\n  opacity: 1;\n  transform: translateY(0);\n  filter: blur(0);\n}\n/* Exit decouples from the stagger: same fade for every line,\n   no Y return, no blur \u2014 so the disappearance reads as a\n   single quiet fade instead of a reverse reveal. */\n.t-stagger.is-hiding .t-stagger-line {\n  opacity: 0;\n  transform: translateY(0);\n  filter: blur(0);\n  transition:\n    opacity 200ms ease,\n    transform 0s linear,\n    filter 0s linear;\n  transition-delay: 0s;\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-stagger-line { transition: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Texts reveal (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\nimport { useRef, useState } from \"react\";\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --stagger-dur: 500ms;\n  --stagger-distance: 12px;\n  --stagger-stagger: 40ms;\n  --stagger-blur: 3px;\n  --stagger-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n}\n\n/* Lines start translated down + blurred + invisible; .is-shown\n   on the parent flips them to their resting state. The second\n   line's transition-delay holds it back by --stagger-stagger\n   so the eye lands on the headline first. */\n.t-stagger-line {\n  display: block;\n  opacity: 0;\n  transform: translateY(var(--stagger-distance));\n  filter: blur(var(--stagger-blur));\n  transition:\n    opacity   var(--stagger-dur) var(--stagger-ease),\n    transform var(--stagger-dur) var(--stagger-ease),\n    filter    var(--stagger-dur) var(--stagger-ease);\n  will-change: transform, opacity, filter;\n}\n.t-stagger-line--2 { transition-delay: var(--stagger-stagger); }\n\n.t-stagger.is-shown .t-stagger-line {\n  opacity: 1;\n  transform: translateY(0);\n  filter: blur(0);\n}\n/* Exit decouples from the stagger: same fade for every line,\n   no Y return, no blur \u2014 so the disappearance reads as a\n   single quiet fade instead of a reverse reveal. */\n.t-stagger.is-hiding .t-stagger-line {\n  opacity: 0;\n  transform: translateY(0);\n  filter: blur(0);\n  transition:\n    opacity 200ms ease,\n    transform 0s linear,\n    filter 0s linear;\n  transition-delay: 0s;\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-stagger-line { transition: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p18\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p18\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// Two states: idle \u2192 shown (staggered entrance) and shown \u2192 hiding\n// (quick fade-out, no Y return). The hiding class is held only long\n// enough for the 200ms fade to play, then dropped so the next show\n// can replay the staggered entrance from a clean state.\nexport function StaggerReveal({ primary, secondary }) {\n  const [shown, setShown]   = useState(false);\n  const [hiding, setHiding] = useState(false);\n  const timerRef = useRef(null);\n\n  const onAnimate = () => {\n    if (shown) {\n      // shown \u2192 hiding (fade out in place)\n      setShown(false);\n      setHiding(true);\n      if (timerRef.current) window.clearTimeout(timerRef.current);\n      timerRef.current = window.setTimeout(() => setHiding(false), 200);\n    } else {\n      // idle \u2192 shown (replay the staggered entrance)\n      if (timerRef.current) window.clearTimeout(timerRef.current);\n      setHiding(false);\n      // setState is async \u2014 flip to shown on the next tick so React\n      // doesn't batch idle\u2192shown into a single render and skip the\n      // entrance transition.\n      window.requestAnimationFrame(() => setShown(true));\n    }\n  };\n\n  const cls = \"t-stagger\" +\n    (shown  ? \" is-shown\"  : \"\") +\n    (hiding ? \" is-hiding\" : \"\");\n\n  return (\n    <>\n      <button type=\"button\" onClick={onAnimate}>Animate</button>\n      <div className={cls}>\n        <strong className=\"t-stagger-line t-stagger-line--1\">{primary}</strong>\n        <span    className=\"t-stagger-line t-stagger-line--2\">{secondary}</span>\n      </div>\n    </>\n  );\n}\n"
 },
 {
  "slug": "tabs-sliding",
  "name": "Tabs sliding",
  "sub": "Pill indicator follows the active tab",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Tabs sliding */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <div class=\"t-tabs\" role=\"tablist\">\n      <span class=\"t-tabs-pill\" aria-hidden=\"true\"></span>\n      <button class=\"t-tab\" role=\"tab\" aria-selected=\"true\">Plan</button>\n      <button class=\"t-tab\" role=\"tab\" aria-selected=\"false\">Debug</button>\n      <button class=\"t-tab\" role=\"tab\" aria-selected=\"false\">Ask</button>\n    </div>\n\n  Wire-up:\n    On click, flip aria-selected on each tab and write the\n    active tab's offsetLeft / offsetWidth onto the pill:\n      pill.style.transform = `translateX(${tab.offsetLeft}px)`;\n      pill.style.width     = `${tab.offsetWidth}px`;\n    On first paint and resize, write the same values WITHOUT\n    a transition (suspend with `transition: none`, force a\n    reflow, restore) so the pill snaps to position before any\n    animation can run.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --tabs-dur: 250ms;\n  --tabs-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --tabs-text-muted: rgba(15, 15, 15, 0.8);\n  --tabs-text-active: #0f0f0f;\n  --tabs-bar-bg: #f1f1f1;\n  --tabs-pill-bg: #ffffff;\n}\n\n/* The bar is just a flex container with padding for the pill\n   to sit inside. Tabs sit on z-index: 1, the pill on z-index: 0,\n   so labels read above the pill background. */\n.t-tabs {\n  position: relative;\n  display: inline-flex;\n  align-items: center;\n  gap: 3px;\n  padding: 3px;\n  border-radius: 48px;\n  background: var(--tabs-bar-bg);\n}\n.t-tab {\n  position: relative;\n  appearance: none;\n  border: 0;\n  background: transparent;\n  height: 30px;\n  padding: 4px 12px;\n  color: var(--tabs-text-muted);\n  cursor: pointer;\n  border-radius: 48px;\n  z-index: 1;\n  transition: color var(--tabs-dur) var(--tabs-ease);\n}\n.t-tab:not([aria-selected=\"true\"]):hover,\n.t-tab[aria-selected=\"true\"] {\n  color: var(--tabs-text-active);\n}\n\n/* The pill: width + transform are written inline by JS so\n   the transition tweens between the previous and next\n   measured positions. */\n.t-tabs-pill {\n  position: absolute;\n  top: 3px;\n  left: 0;\n  height: 30px;\n  width: 0;\n  background: var(--tabs-pill-bg);\n  border-radius: 48px;\n  transform: translateX(0);\n  transition:\n    transform var(--tabs-dur) var(--tabs-ease),\n    width     var(--tabs-dur) var(--tabs-ease);\n  will-change: transform, width;\n  z-index: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-tabs-pill, .t-tab { transition: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Tabs sliding (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\nimport { useEffect, useRef, useState } from \"react\";\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --tabs-dur: 250ms;\n  --tabs-ease: cubic-bezier(0.22, 1, 0.36, 1);\n  --tabs-text-muted: rgba(15, 15, 15, 0.8);\n  --tabs-text-active: #0f0f0f;\n  --tabs-bar-bg: #f1f1f1;\n  --tabs-pill-bg: #ffffff;\n}\n\n/* The bar is just a flex container with padding for the pill\n   to sit inside. Tabs sit on z-index: 1, the pill on z-index: 0,\n   so labels read above the pill background. */\n.t-tabs {\n  position: relative;\n  display: inline-flex;\n  align-items: center;\n  gap: 3px;\n  padding: 3px;\n  border-radius: 48px;\n  background: var(--tabs-bar-bg);\n}\n.t-tab {\n  position: relative;\n  appearance: none;\n  border: 0;\n  background: transparent;\n  height: 30px;\n  padding: 4px 12px;\n  color: var(--tabs-text-muted);\n  cursor: pointer;\n  border-radius: 48px;\n  z-index: 1;\n  transition: color var(--tabs-dur) var(--tabs-ease);\n}\n.t-tab:not([aria-selected=\"true\"]):hover,\n.t-tab[aria-selected=\"true\"] {\n  color: var(--tabs-text-active);\n}\n\n/* The pill: width + transform are written inline by JS so\n   the transition tweens between the previous and next\n   measured positions. */\n.t-tabs-pill {\n  position: absolute;\n  top: 3px;\n  left: 0;\n  height: 30px;\n  width: 0;\n  background: var(--tabs-pill-bg);\n  border-radius: 48px;\n  transform: translateX(0);\n  transition:\n    transform var(--tabs-dur) var(--tabs-ease),\n    width     var(--tabs-dur) var(--tabs-ease);\n  will-change: transform, width;\n  z-index: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-tabs-pill, .t-tab { transition: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p16\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p16\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// The pill's transform + width are written inline by JS so the\n// CSS transition tweens between the previous and next measured\n// positions. We re-snap (no animation) on resize so a viewport\n// change doesn't desync the pill from its tab.\nexport function SlidingTabs({ tabs }) {\n  const rootRef = useRef(null);\n  const pillRef = useRef(null);\n  const tabRefs = useRef([]);\n  const [active, setActive] = useState(0);\n\n  const moveTo = (idx, animate) => {\n    const tab = tabRefs.current[idx];\n    const pill = pillRef.current;\n    if (!tab || !pill) return;\n    const left = tab.offsetLeft;\n    const width = tab.offsetWidth;\n    if (!animate) {\n      const prev = pill.style.transition;\n      pill.style.transition = \"none\";\n      pill.style.transform = `translateX(${left}px)`;\n      pill.style.width = `${width}px`;\n      void pill.offsetWidth;\n      pill.style.transition = prev;\n    } else {\n      pill.style.transform = `translateX(${left}px)`;\n      pill.style.width = `${width}px`;\n    }\n  };\n\n  useEffect(() => {\n    const id = window.requestAnimationFrame(() => moveTo(active, false));\n    const onResize = () => moveTo(active, false);\n    window.addEventListener(\"resize\", onResize);\n    return () => {\n      window.cancelAnimationFrame(id);\n      window.removeEventListener(\"resize\", onResize);\n    };\n  }, [active]);\n\n  return (\n    <div ref={rootRef} className=\"t-tabs\" role=\"tablist\">\n      <span ref={pillRef} className=\"t-tabs-pill\" aria-hidden=\"true\" />\n      {tabs.map((label, i) => (\n        <button\n          key={label}\n          ref={(el) => { tabRefs.current[i] = el; }}\n          type=\"button\"\n          className=\"t-tab\"\n          role=\"tab\"\n          aria-selected={i === active}\n          onClick={() => { setActive(i); moveTo(i, true); }}\n        >\n          {label}\n        </button>\n      ))}\n    </div>\n  );\n}\n"
 },

 {
  "slug": "shimmer-text",
  "name": "Shimmer text",
  "sub": "Masked gradient sweep across text",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Shimmer text */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <!-- Duplicate the visible string into data-text so the\n         ::before layer can mask the gradient onto the same\n         glyphs. Keep them in sync if the text changes. -->\n    <span class=\"t-shimmer\" data-text=\"Planning next moves\">\n      Planning next moves\n    </span>\n\n  Pure CSS \u2014 no JS, no class toggling. Tune --shimmer-base /\n  --shimmer-highlight in your own theme rules so the colors\n  follow light / dark mode.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --shimmer-dur: 2000ms;\n  --shimmer-base: #7c7c7c;\n  --shimmer-highlight: #0d0d0d;\n  --shimmer-band: 400%;\n  --shimmer-ease: linear;\n}\n\n/* Two-layer construction:\n   1. The base text renders normally in --shimmer-base.\n   2. ::before duplicates it via content: attr(data-text),\n      paints a transparent \u2192 highlight \u2192 transparent gradient\n      onto it, and clips that gradient to the glyphs via\n      background-clip: text. Animating background-position\n      sweeps the band across the text. */\n.t-shimmer {\n  position: relative;\n  display: inline-block;\n  color: var(--shimmer-base);\n}\n.t-shimmer::before {\n  content: attr(data-text);\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  background-image: linear-gradient(\n    90deg,\n    transparent          0%,\n    transparent         40%,\n    var(--shimmer-highlight) 50%,\n    transparent         60%,\n    transparent        100%\n  );\n  background-size: var(--shimmer-band) 100%;\n  background-repeat: no-repeat;\n  -webkit-background-clip: text;\n  background-clip: text;\n  color: transparent;\n  -webkit-text-fill-color: transparent;\n  animation: t-shimmer var(--shimmer-dur) var(--shimmer-ease) infinite;\n}\n@keyframes t-shimmer {\n  0%   { background-position: 100% 0; }\n  100% { background-position: 0% 0; }\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-shimmer::before { animation: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Shimmer text (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --shimmer-dur: 2000ms;\n  --shimmer-base: #7c7c7c;\n  --shimmer-highlight: #0d0d0d;\n  --shimmer-band: 400%;\n  --shimmer-ease: linear;\n}\n\n/* Two-layer construction:\n   1. The base text renders normally in --shimmer-base.\n   2. ::before duplicates it via content: attr(data-text),\n      paints a transparent \u2192 highlight \u2192 transparent gradient\n      onto it, and clips that gradient to the glyphs via\n      background-clip: text. Animating background-position\n      sweeps the band across the text. */\n.t-shimmer {\n  position: relative;\n  display: inline-block;\n  color: var(--shimmer-base);\n}\n.t-shimmer::before {\n  content: attr(data-text);\n  position: absolute;\n  inset: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  background-image: linear-gradient(\n    90deg,\n    transparent          0%,\n    transparent         40%,\n    var(--shimmer-highlight) 50%,\n    transparent         60%,\n    transparent        100%\n  );\n  background-size: var(--shimmer-band) 100%;\n  background-repeat: no-repeat;\n  -webkit-background-clip: text;\n  background-clip: text;\n  color: transparent;\n  -webkit-text-fill-color: transparent;\n  animation: t-shimmer var(--shimmer-dur) var(--shimmer-ease) infinite;\n}\n@keyframes t-shimmer {\n  0%   { background-position: 100% 0; }\n  100% { background-position: 0% 0; }\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-shimmer::before { animation: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p15\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p15\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// Pure CSS \u2014 no hooks, no state. The `data-text` attribute\n// duplicates the visible string into the masked ::before layer.\n// Keep them in sync if the text changes.\nexport function Shimmer({ children }) {\n  return (\n    <span className=\"t-shimmer\" data-text={children}>\n      {children}\n    </span>\n  );\n}\n"
 },
 {
  "slug": "tooltip-open-close",
  "name": "Tooltip open/close",
  "sub": "Appear-only delay, instant exit",
  "pro": false,
  "css": "/* Transitions.dev \u2014 Tooltip open/close */\n\n/*\n  Usage:\n    <span class=\"t-tt-wrap\">\n      <button class=\"t-tt-trigger\" aria-describedby=\"tt-1\">\u2026</button>\n      <span class=\"t-tt\" id=\"tt-1\" role=\"tooltip\">Tooltip text</span>\n    </span>\n\n  Pure CSS \u2014 no JS. The wrap (not the trigger) is the hover\n  target so the pointer can drift onto the tooltip without\n  flicker. transition-delay only applies in the hover/focus\n  rule, so leaving snaps it to 0 and the disappear plays\n  immediately. Trigger styling is yours; only the tooltip\n  positioning + its transition live here.\n*/\n\n:root {\n  --tt-in-dur: 150ms;\n  --tt-out-dur: 50ms;\n  --tt-scale: 0.98;\n  --tt-delay: 80ms;\n  --tt-in-ease: ease-out;\n  --tt-out-ease: ease-out;\n  --tt-bg: #ffffff;\n  --tt-fg: #2f2f2f;\n}\n\n.t-tt-wrap {\n  position: relative;\n  display: inline-block;\n}\n.t-tt {\n  position: absolute;\n  bottom: calc(100% + 8px);\n  left: 50%;\n  transform: translate(-50%, 0) scale(var(--tt-scale));\n  transform-origin: 50% 100%;\n  padding: 8px 12px;\n  border-radius: 8px;\n  background: var(--tt-bg);\n  color: var(--tt-fg);\n  white-space: nowrap;\n  box-shadow:\n    0 0 0 1px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.06),\n    0 2px 6px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.05),\n    0 4px 42px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.06);\n  opacity: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  /* Default rule controls the LEAVE state. transition-delay\n     stays unset so leaving plays without delay. */\n  transition:\n    opacity   var(--tt-out-dur) var(--tt-out-ease),\n    transform var(--tt-out-dur) var(--tt-out-ease);\n}\n/* The 50ms delay belongs ONLY to the hover rule so leaving\n   the trigger snaps the delay back to 0 and the disappear\n   plays immediately. */\n.t-tt-wrap:hover .t-tt,\n.t-tt-trigger:focus-visible + .t-tt {\n  opacity: 1;\n  transform: translate(-50%, 0) scale(1);\n  transition-duration: var(--tt-in-dur);\n  transition-timing-function: var(--tt-in-ease);\n  transition-delay: var(--tt-delay);\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-tt { transition: none !important; }\n}",
  "react": "// Transitions.dev \u2014 Tooltip open/close (React, self-contained)\n// Drop into any React project \u2014 no extra CSS file needed.\n\n// \u2500\u2500 Styles \u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\u2500\n// Auto-injected on first import. Idempotent (guarded by\n// the element id) and SSR-safe (no-ops without document).\nconst __TRANSITION_STYLES = `\n:root {\n  --tt-in-dur: 150ms;\n  --tt-out-dur: 50ms;\n  --tt-scale: 0.98;\n  --tt-delay: 80ms;\n  --tt-in-ease: ease-out;\n  --tt-out-ease: ease-out;\n  --tt-bg: #ffffff;\n  --tt-fg: #2f2f2f;\n}\n\n.t-tt-wrap {\n  position: relative;\n  display: inline-block;\n}\n.t-tt {\n  position: absolute;\n  bottom: calc(100% + 8px);\n  left: 50%;\n  transform: translate(-50%, 0) scale(var(--tt-scale));\n  transform-origin: 50% 100%;\n  padding: 8px 12px;\n  border-radius: 8px;\n  background: var(--tt-bg);\n  color: var(--tt-fg);\n  white-space: nowrap;\n  box-shadow:\n    0 0 0 1px rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.06),\n    0 2px 6px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.05),\n    0 4px 42px 0 rgba(0, 0, 0, 0.06);\n  opacity: 0;\n  pointer-events: none;\n  /* Default rule controls the LEAVE state. transition-delay\n     stays unset so leaving plays without delay. */\n  transition:\n    opacity   var(--tt-out-dur) var(--tt-out-ease),\n    transform var(--tt-out-dur) var(--tt-out-ease);\n}\n/* The 50ms delay belongs ONLY to the hover rule so leaving\n   the trigger snaps the delay back to 0 and the disappear\n   plays immediately. */\n.t-tt-wrap:hover .t-tt,\n.t-tt-trigger:focus-visible + .t-tt {\n  opacity: 1;\n  transform: translate(-50%, 0) scale(1);\n  transition-duration: var(--tt-in-dur);\n  transition-timing-function: var(--tt-in-ease);\n  transition-delay: var(--tt-delay);\n}\n\n@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {\n  .t-tt { transition: none !important; }\n}\n`;\nif (typeof document !== \"undefined\" && !document.getElementById(\"transitions-p17\")) {\n  const __style = document.createElement(\"style\");\n  __style.id = \"transitions-p17\";\n  __style.textContent = __TRANSITION_STYLES;\n  document.head.appendChild(__style);\n}\n\n// Pair with the CSS from the CSS tab.\n// Pure CSS \u2014 hover / focus state lives entirely in selectors.\n// Bring your own trigger styling.\nexport function Tooltip({ id, content, children }) {\n  return (\n    <span className=\"t-tt-wrap\">\n      {/* The trigger needs `aria-describedby` pointed at the\n          tooltip's id; CSS uses :focus-visible + sibling selector\n          so the tooltip also shows on keyboard focus. */}\n      {children({ \"aria-describedby\": id, className: \"t-tt-trigger\" })}\n      <span id={id} className=\"t-tt\" role=\"tooltip\">{content}</span>\n    </span>\n  );\n}\n"
